L2076: 5.0 Assessment: While the development of two CGRP antagonists, telcagepant and MK-3207, stopped due to hepatotoxicity, we see little evidence of DILI in this NDA for Atogepant. Two other in-class agents (rimegepant and ubrogepant) were approved without significant liver injury concerns. These later generation agents may carry a lower DILI risk. However, the two approvals were recent (2019 and 2020).
L2077: In vitro, in silico, and animal studies in this NDA did not raise significant liver injury concerns. Three animal models (mouse, rat and monkey) dosing Atogepant for 3, 6 and 9 months respectively did not show histopathologic liver injury. Using in vitro data, quantitative systems toxicology studies (in silico) predicted less injury risk compared to telcagepant and MK-3207. The in silico and in vitro modeling predicted an increased risk of mild ALT elevation beginning at twice the highest Atogepant dosing used in the registration trials.
L2078: Avoiding the use of Atogepant in patients with severe hepatic impairment may be prudent because the in-silico data suggest a dose response for DILI. Hepatic impairment studies showed a 38% increase in systemic exposure in severe hepatic impairment. Also, patients with severe impairment have less tolerance of DILI.
L2079: Registration trial data for this NDA did not suggest significant liver injury risk. Treatment duration ranged from 12 to 52 weeks and dosing from 10 mg daily to 60 mg twice daily. Total number of patients exposed was large at over 1300. There were no obvious imbalances in elevation of ALT or AST elevations between placebo and treatment arms. There were no cases meeting Hy's Law criteria. Case level analyses found just 2 patients with probable DILI due to Atogepant and 4 possible cases. No cases had abnormal bilirubin levels. The 4 possible cases had competing etiologies to explain the liver enzyme elevations. Both patients with probable DILI had short latencies, were asymptomatic and had quick resolution in enzyme elevations after drug stop. The patient who died with prior elevations in ALT and AST, had a myopathy causing these elevations, not DILI. These elevations were resolving when she died of sepsis.
L2080: Therefore, we did not find a liver injury risk that should prevent approval, but the risk is not zero. While the registration trials were large, wider use in the market can uncover a rare risk of significant DILI not captured in these trials. We do not recommend routine monitoring of liver enzymes, but we suggest mentioning potential for ALT or AST elevation in the label. It may be prudent to avoid use in patients with severe hepatic impairment.
L2081: 5.1 Recommendations
L2082: a. Routine blood tests for liver enzyme monitoring is not necessary.b. Consider avoiding use of Atogepant in patients with severe hepatic impairment.c. Consider the wording below for labeling:
L2083: Liver Enzyme Elevations –

L2086: 6.0 Addendum 1: After completing this consultation on May 16, 2021, the sponsor sent a 120-Day Safety Report (Sq 17, SDN 17, 1.11.3) on May 19, 2021. The report covered safety data through Jan 31, 2021.
L2087: Report Review Summary: After review of the report, our assessment and recommendations in Section 5.0 are largely unchanged. However, the report raises questions about an increased DILI risk in Japanese, or Asians in general. It appears that the proportion of patients with elevation in transaminases is higher in Japanese in a Phase 3 study that is not included in the ISS Group 1 or 2 of the NDA. Precise proportions of patients with elevated transaminases by race are not provided, and we would like such data in this Phase 3 and the ISS. Details of our report review are below. Upon our suggestion, DN2 sent an information request on Jun 2, 2021.
L2088: Full Report Review: The Safety Report has two areas of interest: (1) Updated data for the ISS Group 2 and (2) New cases of liver enzyme elevations in 2 studies outside the ISS Groups.
L2089: 6.1 ISS Group 2 OLE Studies: Updated eDISH plot did not find any cases in Hy's Law quadrant. Proportions of patients with transaminases over 3x ULN and higher were not significantly changed from earlier data sent with the NDA. Just 1 new case ( (b) (6), Study 3101-309-002) required review with AST elevation over 3x ULN. We reviewed the case narrative and graphic. This was not DILI. AST elevation occurred 324 days after AGPT start (60 mg/d). The rise was concurrent with increased physical activity and CPK of 4806 U/L. ALT was 38 U/L and bilirubin 0.2 mg/dL. Patient also reported recent increase in alcohol intake. CPK and AST fell to normal within 30 days. AGPT was continued without dose change.
L2090: 6.2 Cases from Studies Not Included in Group 2 OLE Studies: The report describes 5 new case of liver enzyme elevations. No cases met Hy's Law liver test elevation criteria. No case developed jaundice. All recovered.
L2091: 6.2.1 Study CGP-PK-14 (Phase 1 study of PK in health volunteers, IND 114780, Sq 172, 5.3.3.4): One case had elevation in ALT and AST more than 3x ULN after taking AGPT, 60 mg/d, for 7 days. This
L2092: case's narrative and graphic data were reviewed and assessed as probable DILI due to AGPT. However, this reviewer could not find overall study safety data. Target enrollment is 50 healthy volunteers of which 25 would get AGPT at varying doses.
L2093: 6.2.2 Study 3101-306-002 (OLE of Phase 3 RCT, 3101-303-002, IND 114870, Seq 172, 5.3.5.1). Four cases developed elevation in ALT and/or AST greater than 3x ULN. All four were Japanese. We assessed 2 as probable DILI due to AGPT, 1 possible and 1 unlikely. An independent Causality Assessment Committee (CAC) assessed them as 3 probable and 1 unlikely, while the sponsor's internal review assessed them as 3 possible and 1 unlikely. Target enrollment for this Phase 3, 3101-303-002 was 750, randomized 2:1, AGPT to placebo, but this reviewer could not find summary safety information, current enrollment or demographics in this OLE. The sponsor did not provide proportions of patients with elevation in transaminases by race.

However, we found no summary data of proportions of patients with liver enzyme elevations by Japanese versus other races in study 3101-303-002 or other studies.

L2113: Because the sponsor is hoping to market AGPT in Japan, a Phase 1 RCT specifically looking at PK in Japanese versus Caucasians was done in 2017 (Study 3101-101-002, Sq 18, 5.3.3.3). 40 Japanese and 10 Caucasians enrolled. The Japanese were randomized 4:1 to varying exposures of AGPT and placebo. There were no cases of transaminase elevations above 3x ULN in any patient, Japanese or Caucasian (Clinical Study Report, page 251).
L2114: 7.0 Addendum 2: After our review of the 120 Day Safety Report (Sq 17, SDN 17) outlined in Section 6.0, an information request (IR) to assess potential increased DILI risk in Japanese and/or Asians in general was sent by email on Jun 2, 2021 with 4 specific requests. The sponsor's response came on Jun 7, 2021 (Seq 19, SDN 19, 1.11.3). Of note, the response had safety data through May 26, 2021, so the same data are also contained in the Safety Report submitted June 30, 2021 (Sq 22, SDN 22, 5.3.5.3).
L2115: 7.1 Response Review Summary: In totality, the data in the sponsor's response do not warrant labeling a difference in liver injury risk for Japanese or Asians. While the ongoing OLE study of 113 Japanese patients so far enrolled (3101-306-002) indicates a higher rate of transaminase elevation than the overall rate in this NDA's ISS, no such increase is seen for Asians in the ISS nor the RCT from which patients entered 3101-306-002. We acknowledge that the number of Asians in the ISS is too low to draw conclusions. However, the RCT had 248 Asians randomized across 3 arms (placebo, AGPT 30 mg bid,
L2116: AGPT 60 mg/d) for 12 weeks of treatment. Only 1 of 248 Asians had elevation in transaminases which was unlikely attributable to the IP. The RCT remains blinded. At least 113 of those 248 were Japanese and enrolled in 3101-306-002. This OLE has no comparator arm. Therefore, we cannot definitively assign an increased risk of DILI amongst Japanese, or Asians in general, based on these data.
L2117: Similarly, we do not think labeling for a possible difference in DILI risk for blacks is needed. There was a higher rate of transaminase elevations in blacks on AGPT in the Group 2 ISS, but the number of blacks on placebo is small at 38 and may not offer a large enough comparator arm. The number of blacks in the Group 1 ISS was larger (69 placebo vs. 223 AGPT), and no clear differences were seen in transaminase elevations amongst blacks. Therefore, the data are equivocal.
L2118: Our detailed review follows (Section 7.2)
L2119: 7.2 Sponsor Response Review:
L2120: 7.2.1 Response to request 1: We had requested clarification whether the ongoing, 52-week, open label study 3101-306-002 is restricted to Japanese nationals. The sponsor replied that it is so restricted. Therefore, the overall rates of enzyme elevations and DILI cases for this study reflect Japanese nationality rates and can be compared to other race or nationality groups in the ISS.7.2.2 Response to request 2: We requested current enrollment status of 3101-306-002. The sponsor replied that 113 patients had enrolled and included in the safety population as of May 26, 2021. Therefore, we presume that all 113 have taken at least one dose of AGPT at 60 mg/d. There is no comparator arm in this study.7.2.3 Response to request 3: We requested elevation in liver test rates for Study 3101-306-002 in tabular form. The sponsor sent the table below. The rates of ALT and AST elevations are higher compared to ISS Group 2 of 1198 patients also on 60 mg/d (see Section 4.5, Table 4 above). For example, the rate for ALT or AST elevation &gt;3x and &gt;5x ULN were 2.7% to 4.4% and 1.8% to 2.7% for Japanese nationals, respectively. The rates of elevations in ALT or AST at the same multiples of ULN were 1.4% and 0.7% for ISS Group 2.

L2150: 1 Summary
L2151: This review evaluates statistically the data of the 2-year oral carcinogenicity study of AGN-241689 in Wistar Han Rats and 2-year oral carcinogenicity study in mice. The review analyzes the dose-response relationship of tumor incidences and mortality (including tumor-related mortality). The analyses results showed that AGN-241689 had no effects on survival or tumor incidence in either species.

L2155: Rat Study: Rats (60/sex/group) were dosed by oral gavage with AGN-241689 once daily for up to 104 weeks. The respective AGN-241689 doses in the water control (WC), vehicle control (VC), low (LD), mid (MD), and high-dose (HD) groups were 0, 0, 10, 20, and 100 mg/kg/day for male rats and 0, 0, 25, 65, and 200 mg/kg/day for female rats.
L2156: The survival analyses didn't show any statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality in both males and females. The respective survival rates in the WC, VC, LD, MD, or HD of AGN-241689 at the time they were terminated were 72%, 75%, 70%, 70%, and 72% in males and 62%, 63%, 72%, 62%, and 72% in females.
L2157: Tumor analysis results (Table 1) showed that the positive dose response was statistically significant in the incidence of rare benign polyp tumor in the cervix in females when compared with vehicle control group (0.0154 &lt;0.025). For this tumor, the incidence rate of any treated group was not statistically significantly greater than that of the vehicle control group or water control group. For this tumor type in combined organ of cervix and uterus, the positive dose response was not statistically significant.
L2158: Mouse Study: Mice (60/sex/group) were dosed by oral gavage with AGN-241689 once daily for up to 104 weeks. The respective AGN-241689 doses in the water control (WC), vehicle control (VC), low (LD), mid (MD), and high-dose (HD) groups were 0, 0, 5, 20, and 75 mg/kg/day for male mice and 0, 0, 5, 30, and 160 mg/kg/day for female mice.
L2159: File Name: NDA215206Carcin_Primary_2.doc
L2160: The survival analyses didn't show any statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality in both males and females. The respective survival rates in the WC, VC, LD, MD, or HD of AGN-241689 at the time they were terminated were 47%, 50%, 62%, 53%, and 50% in males and 48%, 48%, 57%, 55%, and 58% in females.
L2161: Tumor analysis results (Table 1) showed that the positive dose response was statistically significant in the incidence of rare malignant rhabdomyosarcoma tumor in subcutis in male mice (p=0.0164 &lt; 0.025) when compared with water control group. For this tumor type, the incidence rate of any treated group was not statistically significantly greater than that of the vehicle control group or water control group.
L2162: 2 Background
L2163: The sponsor, AbbVie Inc., is submitting, under section 505(b)(1) of the Federal Food, Drug, and Cosmetic Act and 21 CFR 314.50, a New Drug Application (NDA) for atogepant tablets, 10 mg, 30 mg, and 60 mg. The proposed indication is for the preventive treatment of migraine in adults with &lt; 15 migraine days per month. The sponsor submitted two carcinogenicity study reports: Study cgp-tx-06-a1: “A 2-Year Carcinogenicity Study by Oral Gavage in Wistar Han Rat” and Study cgp-tx-05-a1: “A 2-Year Carcinogenicity Study by Oral Gavage in the Mouse”. The study reports and the electronic SEND datasets were submitted on 1/28/2021 (via NDA 215206).
L2164: The phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component of the effect of treatment, and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor incidence rate as dose increases. Results of this review have been discussed with the nonclinical team.

L2173: Assessment of carcinogenicity was based on anatomic pathology to include incidences of masses or tumors and possible effects on survival. Mortality, clinical observations, body weight, and food consumption were also monitored and evaluated.
L2174: 3.1 Sponsor's Analyses
L2175: 3.1.1 Survival Analysis
L2176: Intercurrent mortality data were analyzed using the Kaplan-Meier product-limit method. An overall test comparing all groups was conducted using a log-rank test12. Any animal with accidental injury that caused death or unscheduled sacrifice was censored in the estimation. In addition, all animals still alive at the end of the experimental period were censored at the following day. If this overall test was significant (p &lt; 0.05) and there were more than two groups, then a follow up analysis was done where each treatment group was compared to the control group using a log-rank test. Results of all pair-wise comparisons are reported at the 0.05 and 0.01 significance levels. All endpoints were analyzed using two-tailed tests.
L2177: Sponsor's concluded results: No AGN-241689-related causes of death occurred in either sex in this study.
L2178: 3.1.2 Tumor Data Analysis
L2179: The statistical evaluation of tumor data was limited to subcutis and hemolymphoreticular tissue using all study animals, to all non-secondary neoplastic lesions found in Study Plan-required tissues/sites, and to the combination of hemangiosarcoma findings across whole body.
L2180: Palpable neoplastic lesions found under skin, subcutis, and study plan-required glands were statistically analyzed in a “mortality independent” context according to Peto’s onset rate using all study animals. Whereas, non-palpable neoplastic findings were statistically analyzed in a “mortality dependent” context according to Peto’s prevalence and death rate methods.
L2181: For each dataset of interest, a one-tailed Peto’s survival-adjusted trend test, using dose level scores, was conducted in order to assess significance of tumor rate increase across vehicle control group (Group 2) and test item treated groups. In addition, a two-tailed Peto’s was used to compare the incidence rates of Group 2 and water control group (Group 1).
L2182: If the result revealed significant difference between Groups 1 and 2, then an upper-tailed Peto's trend test was used in order to assess significance of tumor rate increase in each of the test item treated groups when compared to each of Groups 1 and 2. Otherwise, an upper-tailed Peto's trend test was used in order to assess significance of tumor rate increase in each of the test item treated groups when only compared to Group 2.
L2183: As per Lin (Lin, K.K. 1997), the discrete permutation distribution tests for dose responses were used for individual tumor types with 10 or less tumor-bearing animals. As recommended by the FDA (Guidance for Industry, 2001), the overall trend and the pairwise comparison tests were discussed in the main report according to the tumor prevalence classification (common or rare).
L2184: The Peto (Peto R, Pike MC, Day NE et al, 1980) scoring system was used and assigned to each neoplasm from all animals examined. Histopathology data was submitted separately in an electronic format to Charles River Laboratories, Montreal, Canada for statistical analysis.
L2185: File Name: NDA215206Carcin_Primary_2.doc
L2186: Results of the statistical analysis was provided to the study director for inclusion in the toxicology report.
L2187: All p-values are reported using upper-tailed test, unless otherwise indicated. Evaluation criteria (levels of significance) were applied differently for rare tumors (background rate of 1% or less) and common tumors (background rate greater than 1%).
L2188: Sponsor's concluded results: There were no statistically significant differences between the control and the AGN-241689 treated groups.
L2189: 3.2 Reviewer's Analyses
L2190: To verify the sponsor's analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing pharmacologist, this review analyzed the SAS data sets of these studies received on 1/28/2021 via NDA 215206. The vehicle control group was compared to each of treated group and water control group for the survival analyses and the tumor analyses.
L2191: 3.2.1 Data Quality
L2192: The sponsor only submitted SEND database on 1/28/2021 via NDA 215206. The data quality was acceptable.
L2193: 3.2.2 Survival Analysis
L2194: The survival distributions of rates in all treatment groups were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. For control, low, medium, and high dose groups, the dose response relationship was tested using the likelihood ratio test and the homogeneity of survival distributions were tested using the log-rank test. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rates are given in Figures 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively. The intercurrent mortality data are given in Tables 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively. Results of the tests for dose response relationship and homogeneity of survivals, are given in Tables 3A and 3B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
L2195: Reviewer's findings: This reviewer's analysis showed the numbers (percent) of death that occurred prior to termination of the group were 17 (28%), 15 (25%), 18 (30%), 18 (30%), or 17 (28%) in male rats and 23 (38%), 22 (37%), 17 (28%), 23 (38%), or 17 (28%) in female rats in the WC, VC, LD, MD, or HD groups, respectively. The survival analyses didn't show any statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality in males and females.
L2196: 3.2.3 Tumor Data Analysis
L2197: The tumor data were analyzed for dose response relationships and pairwise comparisons of control group with each of the treated groups. Both the dose response relationship tests and pairwise comparisons were performed using the Poly-k method described in the papers of
L2198: File Name: NDA215206Carcin_Primary_2.doc
L2199: Bailer and Portier [2] and Bieler and Williams [3]. In this method an animal that lives the full study period (w_{max}) or dies before the terminal sacrifice but develops the tumor type being tested gets a score of s_h = 1. An animal that dies at week w_h without developing the tumor
L2200: before the end of the study gets a score of s_h = \left(\frac{w_h}{w_{max}}\right)^k &lt; 1. The adjusted group size is defined as
L2201: \sum s_h. As an interpretation, an animal with score s_h = 1 can be considered as a whole animal while an animal with score s_h &lt; 1 can be considered as a partial animal. The adjusted group size \sum s_h is equal to N (the original group size) if all animals live up to the end of the study or if each animal that dies before the terminal sacrifice develops at least one tumor of the tumor type being tested, otherwise the adjusted group size is less than N. These adjusted group sizes are then used for the dose response relationship (or the pairwise) tests using the Cochran-Armitage test. One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k, which depends on the tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For long term 104-week standard rat and mouse studies, a value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis of this data. For the calculation of p-values the exact permutation method was used.
L2202: The adjusted levels of significance for testing a positive dose response in the 2-year rat study are 0.005 and 0.025 for a common tumor and a rare tumor, respectively. The adjusted levels of significance for the pairwise comparison in the 2-year rat study are 0.01 and 0.05 for a common tumor and a rare tumor, respectively. A rare tumor is defined as one in which the tumor rate is less than 1% in the vehicle control group. The tumor rates and the p-values of the tested tumor types are listed in Tables 5A, and 5B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
L2203: This reviewer also performed a separate set of tumor data analyses to test the positive dose responses in tumor incidence rates among the water control group and the low, medium, and high groups, and to test the pairwise comparisons between the water control group and each of the low, medium, high, and vehicle control groups. Since there were no statistically significant positive responses, and statistically significant differences in tumor incidences between the water control group and the vehicle control group in all the tumor types tested, the results of this separate set of tumor data analyses were not included in this reviewer's report.
L2204: Reviewer's findings: Following table displays the tumor types showing p-values less than or equal to 0.05 either for dose response relationships or for pairwise comparisons of treated groups and control.

L2243: Note: The p-values marked with an asterisk * indicate statistically significant dose responses at 0.005 and 0.025 for a common tumor and a rare tumor, respectively. The p-values marked with an asterisk ** indicate statistically significant pairwise comparison at 0.01 and 0.05 for a common tumor and a rare tumor, respectively.
L2244: Based on the criteria of adjustment for multiple testing discussed above, the positive dose response was statistically significant in the incidence of rare malignant rhabdomyosarcoma tumor in subcutis in males (p=0.0164 &lt; 0.025) when compared with water control group. For this tumor type, the incidence rate of any treated group was not statistically significantly greater than that of the vehicle control group or water control group.
L2245: 5 Conclusion
L2246: This review evaluates statistically the data of the 2-year oral carcinogenicity study of AGN-241689 in Wistar Han Rats and 2-year oral carcinogenicity study in mice. The review analyzes the dose-response relationship of tumor incidences and mortality (including tumor-related mortality). The analyses results showed that AGN-241689 had no effects on survival or tumor incidence in either species.

L2249: &amp; X/ZZ (YY). X=#number of tumor bearing animals; YY=#mortality weighted total number of animals; ZZ=#unweighted total number of animals observed; NS = Not significant. Note: The p-values marked with an asterisk * indicate statistically significant dose responses at 0.005 and 0.025 for a common tumor and a rare tumor, respectively.
L2250: Rat Study: Rats (60/sex/group) were dosed by oral gavage with AGN-241689 once daily for up to 104 weeks. The respective AGN-241689 doses in the water control (WC), vehicle control (VC), low (LD), mid (MD), and high-dose (HD) groups were 0, 0, 10, 20, and 100 mg/kg/day for male rats and 0, 0, 25, 65, and 200 mg/kg/day for female rats.
L2251: The survival analyses didn't show any statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality in both males and females. The respective survival rates in the WC, VC, LD, MD, or HD of AGN-241689 at the time they were terminated were 72%, 75%, 70%, 70%, and 72% in males and 62%, 63%, 72%, 62%, and 72% in females.
L2252: Tumor analysis results (Table 1) showed that the positive dose response was statistically significant in the incidence of rare benign polyp tumor in the cervix in females when compared with vehicle control group (0.0154 &lt;0.025). For this tumor, the incidence rate of any treated group was not statistically significantly greater than that of the vehicle control group or water control group. For this tumor type in combined organ of cervix and uterus, the positive dose response was not statistically significant.
L2253: Mouse Study: Mice (60/sex/group) were dosed by oral gavage with AGN-241689 once daily for up to 104 weeks. The respective AGN-241689 doses in the water control (WC), vehicle
L2254: control (VC), low (LD), mid (MD), and high-dose (HD) groups were 0, 0, 5, 20, and 75 mg/kg/day for male mice and 0, 0, 5, 30, and 160 mg/kg/day for female mice.
L2255: The survival analyses didn't show any statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality in both males and females. The respective survival rates in the WC, VC, LD, MD, or HD of AGN-241689 at the time they were terminated were 47%, 50%, 62%, 53%, and 50% in males and 48%, 48%, 57%, 55%, and 58% in females.
L2256: Tumor analysis results (Table 1) showed that the positive dose response was statistically significant in the incidence of rare malignant rhabdomyosarcoma tumor in subcutis in male mice (p=0.0164 &lt; 0.025) when compared with water control group. For this tumor type, the incidence rate of any treated group was not statistically significantly greater than that of the vehicle control group or water control group.

L2337: Clinical Review Memorandum
L2338: Division of Hepatology and Nutrition, Drug-Induced Liver Injury (DILI) TeamOffice of New DrugsCenter for Drug Evaluation and ResearchFood and Drug Administration
L2339: NDA215206Drug nameAtogepantSponsorAbbVie, IncReview itemIR Response (SDN 19, SN 19)Requesting DivisionDivision of Neurology-2 (DN2)Author namePaul H. Hayashi, MD, MPHDateJul 7, 2021
L2340: I. OVERALL ASSESSMENT AND RECOMMENDATION(S)
L2341: The response data provided by the sponsor does not warrant labeling a difference in liver injury risk for Japanese, or Asians overall. While the ongoing open-label extension (OLE) study (3101-306-002) indicates a higher rate of transaminase elevation than the overall rate in the NDA's Integrated Summary of Safety (ISS), no such increase is seen for Asians in the ISS nor the randomized clinical trial (RCT) from which patients entered 3101-306-002. The rates of transaminase elevations in the OLE are 2.7% for 5x ULN and 1% for &gt;10-20x ULN without any cases of hyperbilirubinemia. The number of Asians in the ISS is too low to draw conclusions. However, the RCT had 248 Asians with only 1 patient having elevation in transaminases which was unlikely attributable to the investigational product (IP). The RCT remains blinded. The OLE has no comparator arm. Therefore, we cannot definitively assign an increased risk of DILI amongst Japanese, or Asians in general, based on these data.
L2342: II. BACKGROUND
L2343: The DILI team has been helping DN2 with assessing DILI risk in this NDA since February 18, 2021. Atogepant is an oral small molecule antagonist of the calcitonin gene related peptide (CGRP) receptor. It is under NDA review for the prevention of migraine headaches in adults. Other agents in this class have had problems with drug-induced liver injury (DILI). We were asked by the Division of Neurology 2 (DN2) to review "patients with abnormal LFTs for potential liver toxicity" associated with Atogepant with particular attention to a severe adverse event that resulted in fatality. We were asked to give opinion on whether investigational product (IP) "carries a risk of potential liver toxicity and, if so, how this may inform labeling."
L2344: Our full written consult is in process, but thus far we have not seen significant liver toxicity potential that threatens approval. At our suggestion, an IR was sent
L2345: on Jun 2, 2021 asking for more information about potential increased risk of DILI amongst Japanese in a trial outside the NDA's ISS. The response arrived on Jun 7, 2021 (SDN 19, SN 19). Of note, the response had safety data through May 26, 2021, so the same data are contained in the Safety Report submitted June 30, 2021 (Sq 22, SDN 22, 5.3.5.3).
L2346: III. SIGNIFICANT REVIEW FINDINGS
L2347: Review of Sponsor Response:
L2348: a. Response to request 1: We had requested clarification whether the ongoing, 52-week, open label study 3101-306-002 is restricted to Japanese nationals. The sponsor replied that it is so restricted. Therefore, the overall rates of enzyme elevations and DILI cases for this study reflect Japanese nationality rates and can be compared to other race or nationality groups in the ISS.b. Response to request 2: We requested current enrollment status of 3101-306-002. The sponsor replied that 113 patients had enrolled and included in the safety population as of May 26, 2021. Therefore, we presume that all 113 have taken at least one dose of AGPT at 60 mg/d. There is no comparator arm in this study.c. Response to request 3: We requested elevation in liver test rates for Study 3101-306-002 in tabular form. See Table 1. The rates of ALT and AST elevations are higher compared to ISS Group 2 of 1198 patients also on 60 mg/d (see Section 4.5, Table 4 above). For example, the rate for ALT or AST elevation &gt;3x and &gt;5x ULN were 2.7% to 4.4% and 1.8% to 2.7% for Japanese nationals, respectively. The rates of elevations in ALT or AST at the same multiples of ULN were 1.4% and 0.7% for ISS Group 2.

L2350: The sponsor also provided an additional case (patient ID: (b) (6), Study 3101-306-002) of transaminase elevation over &gt;3x ULN that occurred between (b) (6). This case was not included in the 120-day report reviewed in Section 6.0. We assessed this case as unlikely DILI due to AGPT. Acetaminophen 1-3 grams IV daily x 6 days is a more likely explanation. AGPT was continued without dose reduction through complete dechallenge. ALT rose to 3-5x ULN without hyperbilirubinemia. This case is included in the table above.
L2351: d. We requested liver enzyme elevation rates by race, particularly Asians, in Groups 1 and 2 of the ISS. We also requested these rates for the blinded, RCT, 3101-303-002, from which Japanese patients entered the open label extension (OLE) study, 3101-306-002. The sponsor provided tables for this request (Seq 19, SDN 19, 1.11.3, pages 11-20).
L2352: Group 1 ISS: There were no enzyme elevations in Asians, but there were only 20 Asians out of 1727 total. Nationalities were not recorded. While blacks on AGPT had higher crude occurrence rate and incidence rates per 100-person year exposure compared to whites the difference between blacks on placebo and AGPT was equivocal. (see table x)

L2358: Study 3101-303-002: In this blinded RCT, the rates of transaminase elevations were low across all races. Only one Asian out of 248 had an ALT 3-5x ULN. No unblinded data were provided. A narrative was provided for the one Asian case. We assessed it as possible to unlikely DILI due to AGPT, though if DILI is present then there was partial tolerance with improvement in liver enzymes while still on IP. Otherwise, fatty liver and autoimmune hepatitis compete well. Transaminases rose to 2.6 to 3.5x ULN at day 14. Patient had gained 3.2 kg from screening to day 14. IP was held on Day 16 and then restarted on Day 28. Transaminases fell to 1.0x to 1.7x ULN through day 56 while still on IP. ANA was 1:40. Other serologic evaluations were negative. Imaging showed fatty liver.
L2359: IV. CONCLUSIONS
L2360: The data in the sponsor's response do not warrant labeling a difference in liver injury risk for Japanese or Asians. While the ongoing OLE study of 113 Japanese patients so far enrolled (3101-306-002) indicates a higher rate of transaminase elevation than the overall rate in this NDA's ISS, no such increase is seen for Asians in the ISS nor the RCT from which patients entered 3101-306-002. We acknowledge that the number of Asians in the ISS is too low to draw conclusions. However, the RCT had 248 Asians randomized across 3 arms (placebo, AGPT 30 mg bid, AGPT 60 mg/d) for 12 weeks of treatment. Only 1 of 248 Asians had elevation in transaminases which was unlikely attributable to the IP. The RCT remains blinded. At least 113 of those 248 were Japanese and enrolled in 3101-306-002. This OLE has no comparator arm. The rates of transaminase elevations in the OLE are 2.7% for 5x ULN and 1% for &gt;20x ULN without any cases of hyperbilirubinemia. Therefore, we cannot definitively assign an increased risk of DILI for Japanese, or Asians, based on these data.
L2361: Similarly, we cannot assign a difference in DILI risk for blacks. There was a higher rate of transaminase elevations in blacks on AGPT in the Group 2 ISS, but the number of blacks on placebo is small at 38 and may not offer a large enough comparator arm. The number of blacks in the Group 1 ISS was larger (69 placebo vs. 223 AGPT), and no clear differences were seen in transaminase elevations amongst blacks. Therefore, the data are equivocal.
L2362: V. RECOMMENDED REGULATORY ACTION(S)
L2363: We do not recommend any regulatory action for this IR response currently. We have informed DN2 of our assessment. We will continue to collaborate with them during their NDA review. Our full written consult assessing DILI risk in this NDA is pending our further review and communication with DN2.

L2372: Clinical Review Memorandum
L2373: Division of Hepatology and Nutrition, Drug-Induced Liver Injury (DILI) TeamOffice of New DrugsCenter for Drug Evaluation and ResearchFood and Drug Administration
L2374: NDA215206Drug nameAtogepantSponsorAbbVie, IncReview item120 Day Clinical/Safety Update (SDN 22, SN 22)Requesting DivisionDivision of Neurology-2 (DN2)Author namePaul H. Hayashi, MD, MPHDateJul 7, 2021
L2375: I. OVERALL ASSESSMENT AND RECOMMENDATION(S)
L2376: This 120-day Safety Update Report included 4 new cases of elevation in transaminases. We assessed 3 as unlikely and 1 as possible DILI due to investigational product (IP). None had hyper bilirubinemia. The possible DILI case is still on IP with monitoring of improved transaminases. No new cases had transaminase elevations over 8x ULN. These data do not significantly change our ongoing assessment of this NDA's DILI risk. We have no recommendations for this report at this time.
L2377: II. BACKGROUND
L2378: The DILI team has been helping DN2 with assessing DILI risk in this NDA since February 18, 2021. Atogepant is an oral small molecule antagonist of the calcitonin gene related peptide (CGRP) receptor. It is under NDA review for the prevention of migraine headaches in adults. Other agents in this class have had problems with drug-induced liver injury (DILI). We were asked by the Division of Neurology 2 (DN2) to review “patients with abnormal LFTs for potential liver toxicity” associated with Atogepant with particular attention to a severe adverse event that resulted in fatality. We were asked to give opinion on whether investigational product (IP) “carries a risk of potential liver toxicity and, if so, how this may inform labeling.”
L2379: Our full written consult is in process, but thus far we have not seen significant liver toxicity potential that threatens approval. Much of the data in this Safety Report submitted June 30, 2021 (Sq 22, SDN 22, 5.3.5.3) appear in the Jun 7, 2021 (SDN 19, SN 19) response to information request (IR), for which we submitted a review also on July 7, 2021. Our full written consultation for regarding DILI risk for this NDA is pending.
L2380: III. SIGNIFICANT REVIEW FINDINGS
L2381: Summary review of 4 cases:
L2382: Adequate narrative and tabular lab data were provided for these four cases. We reviewed all four and our Causality Score assessments are shown below along with other data. Only one was considered possibly due to DILI from AGPT. All cases remained blinded to their treatment assignment in the RCT.

L2384: Liver Test Data for Safety Population.
L2385: The Report also contains liver test elevation data for this overall safety population in several tables (Appendix 2). No significant changes were seen. Study 3101-306-002 which is an OLE study outside this NDA's ISS is ongoing in Japan and has 113 patients thus far enrolled. The amount of transaminase elevation is higher than the overall ISS (see Table 14.3-3.3, SDN 22, SN 22, 5.3.5.3). We already reviewed this issue via an information request (IR) and sponsor response. Please see our review of the sponsor's IR response (July 7, 2021) in DARRTs.
L2386: IV. CONCLUSIONS
L2387: None of the four new cases raise concerns for DILI beyond what we have already seen for this NDA. Tabular data of liver tests elevations for the overall safety population in this report did not have significant changes.
L2388: V. RECOMMENDED REGULATORY ACTION(S)
L2389: We do not recommend regulatory action for this 120-Day Safety Update Report. We will continue to collaborate with DN2 during their NDA review. Our full written consult assessing DILI risk in this NDA is pending our further review and communication with DN2.
